20.1.6 Defining the CNC-Machine components and housing
At the first stage of the CNC-Machine definition, you must add the machine components.
To add different machine components do the following:

        · Click on the Edit Machine icon.
        · Right-click on the machine name and the menu will appear.

              · Add Transl. Axis: This parameter enables you to select and add a translational axis in the machine kinematics
                 from sub-menu. The sub-menu contains X Axis, Y Axis, Z Axis and User Defined options. User Defined option
                 enables you to add a translational axis in the machine kinematics with customized direction.

              · Add Rot. Axis: This parameter enables you to select and add a rotational axis in the machine kinematics from
                 sub-menu. The sub-menu contains A Axis, B Axis, C Axis and User Defined options. User Defined option enables
                 you to add rotational axis in the machine kinematics with customized direction.

              · Add Coord. Transform: This parameter enables you to select a coordinate transform element in the machine
                 kinematics.

256
                                                                                                                 20. Machine Definition

                 · Workpiece Transform: Workpiece Transform option enables you to add a workpiece transform
                     object in the machine kinematics.

                 · Holder Transform: Holder Transform option enables you to add a holder transform object in the
                     machine kinematics.

                 · User Defined: User Defined option enables you to add a customized transform object in the
                     machine kinematics.

· Add Dynamic Element: This parameter enables you to select a dynamic element in the machine kinematics. By
   default, Tool Spindle Revolving Group, Single Workpiece Set and Single Tool Set are available in sub-menu.
   First, select one option from Tool Spindle Revolving Group, Single Workpiece Set and Single Tool Set. When
   you select the Tool Spindle Revolving Group or Single Tool Set option, it adds a holder transform. When you
   select the Single Workpiece Set option, it adds workpiece transform.

                 · Workpiece Spindle Revolving Group: It is defined under the spindle rotation axis and usually, it
                     contains the Fixture and Geometry objects for clamp system (chuck and jaws) and the Workpiece
                     set.

                 · Tool Spindle Revolving Group: It is defined at the end in the kinematic branch. It contains
                     Geometry objects rotating with the tool and the Tool Set.

                 · Single Workpiece Set: This option enables you to add a workpiece set (workpiece adapter
                     containing workpiece, fixture, stock, initial stock and tool path) in the machine kinematics.

                 · Single Tool Set: This option enables you to add a tool set (holder transform containing the tool
                     with all its parts: holder, arbor, shaft and flute) in the machine kinematics.

                 · Workpiece: This option enables you to define a workpiece (Target part) element.

                 · Fixture: This option enables you to define a stock fixture or chuck and jaws (from a clamp system).
                     Usually, the jaws are defined under translation or rotation axis and the chuck is above these, but
                     under the spindle rotation axis and Workpiece Spindle Revolving Group.

                 · Stock: This option enables you to define a stock fixture or chuck and jaws (from a clamp system).
                     Usually, the jaws are defined under translation or rotation axis and the chuck is above these, but
                     under the spindle rotation axis.

                 · Initial Stock: This option enables you to define initial stock element.

                 · Toolpath: This option enables you to define tool path line.

                 · Tool: This option enables you to define the tool element like Flute, Shaft, Arbor and Holder.

     When you click on holder transform or workpiece transform then Workpiece Spindle Revolving Group,
     Workpiece, Fixture, Stock, Initial Stock, Tool path and Tool options are available. The objects, Workpiece,
     Stock, Initial Stock, Toolpath and Tool must be located under a Workpiece Set. Only Fixture objects can appear
     outside a Workpiece Set to define clamp systems.

· Add Geometry: This parameter enables you to add one or more geometries in the machine kinematics.

· Add Tail Sock: This parameter enables you to add a tailstock object in the machine kinematics.

· Add Collision Check: This parameter enables you to define the collision checking elements in two groups. Each
   component from one group is checked with respect to all components from another group. Also, the proximity
   alert functionality depends on the groups created. This parameter contains two options:

                 · Tool-Workpiece/Stock: By default, the Tool-Workpiece/Stock contains workpiece, stock and fixture
                     elements in one group and tools in another group. Also, this option enables you to add or remove
                     elements from groups. You can only add the elements which are not introduced in the machine
                     magazine.
                                                                                                                                              257
                   Tool-Workpiece/Stock option is deactivated when no element in both groups is available. Minimum one element
                   is required in both groups.

                               · User Defined: By default, this collision check option is empty. You can add or remove elements
                                   as per your requirement.

              · Add Magazine: This parameter enables you to add one magazine in the machine kinematics.
              · Add Wire Heads: This parameter enables you to add the wire heads (upper and lower heads) to define Wire

                 Machine.
                               · Add Upper Head: This option enables you to add upper head in the machine kinematics.
                               · Add Lower Head: This option enables you to add lower head in the machine kinematics.

                    The Upper Head is represented by one geometry and tool set. The Lower Head is represented by one geometry.
              · Add Mount Adapter: This option enables you to add a mount adapter in the machine kinematics. It is used to

                 mount heads from the magazine.
                               · Head Adapter: This option enables you to add a head adapter in the machine kinematics.

                               · User Defined: This option enables you to add a user defined mount adapter in the machine
                                   kinematics.

              · Make as Machine Housing: This option allows you to make any normal geometry as machine housing geometry.

20.1.7 Defining the coordinate transformation

In some cases, the CNC-machine spindle is not aligned in the positive Z-axis direction.                      For
example, an angular attachment enables you to transform the vertical spindle rotation (around                the
Z-axis) into the horizontal rotation (around the Y-axis). In this case, the Z-axis of                        the
spindle unit is not parallel to the default main spindle direction.

In the illustration below, the default XYZ coordinates describe the main spindle direction
(coordinate system of the CNC-machine). The X'Y'Z' coordinate system describes the
angular attachment. These coordinate systems have different axes orientations.

                                       Z'-                      Y'-  X'+

                                       X'-                      Y'+  Z'+

                           Y-  Z+  X+

                           X-  Z-  Y+

The coordinate transformation between the default coordinate system and the spindle coordinate system has to be performed
in order to enable correct execution of the tool path by the tool mounted in the spindle unit.

To perform the necessary coordinate transformation, a new coordinate transformation item was added into the CNC-machine
definition tree. Hierarchically, this item has to be "parent" to the holder_transform item.

The element properties table contains a transformation matrix.

Using this matrix, you can define the transformation of the coordinate system. In other words, you can define the orientation
and location of a transformed coordinate system relative (X'Y'Z') to the coordinate system of the CNC-Machine (XYZ).

The columns of this matrix describe the axis orientation of the CNC-Machine coordinate system relative to the transformed
coordinate system. The X column defines the direction of the X-axis, the Y column defines that of the Y-axis, and the Z column
defines that of the Z-axis. The Shift column defines the offset of the transformed coordinate system origin relative to the CNC-
Machine coordinate system origin.

The rows of the matrix define the orientation of the transformed coordinate system relative to the CNC-Machine coordinate
system. The X row defines the direction of the X-axis, the Y row defines that of the Y-axis, and the Z row defines that of the
Z-axis.

By default, the diagonal of the transformation matrix is filled with 1 values. This means that the initial coordinate
system of the CNC-machine and the transformed coordinate system are the same and coordinate transformation
is not performed. Such transformation matrixes were used for the station_transform and adaptor_

transform definition.                                                         Z+                        Y'-

In case a transformation is required, you have to fill the transformation Y-                   X+  Z'-       X'+
matrix.

The transformed coordinate system for the angular attachment discussed X-                      Y+  X'-       Z'+

before is obtained by the 90° rotation of the CNC-Machine coordinate          Z-                        Y'+

system around the X-axis.

                                                                             CNC machine              Transformed
                                                                          coordinate system        coordinate system

                                                                                                             259
The resulting transformation is as follows:                                   X'(X=+1)
                                                 Z(Y'=-1)

                                                            X(X'=+1)

     Y(Z'=+1)                                                                    Z'(Y=+1)
                                                                      Y'(Z=-1)

        CNC machine                                                      Transformed
     coordinate system                                                coordinate system

20.1.8 Defining the translational axis

When all the non-moving components of the CNC-Machine are defined, you have to define the moving components. Moving
components have to be defined according to their hierarchical "parents-children" dependencies. The order of the definition is
the following: "parents" have to be defined before the "children". In case of Table-Table machines, the first component to be
defined is the sliding carriage that performs the X-axis movements and then the spindle unit that moves along the Z-axis. These
two components are joined into a separate set; the movements of this set are independent from the movements of Y-, B- and
C-axes.

Click the X item in the CNC-Machine definition tree. In the element properties table, the orientation of the axis is defined by a
vector with three coordinates. The following limits are set for the axis: the Min Limit value is -380 and the Max Limit value is 380.

Take a note that the same values must be set in the corresponding VMID file.

260
                                                                                                                                 20. Machine Definition
Click the Z item. Notice that the Z-axis is a "child" of the X-axis. The following limits are set for the Z-axis: the Min Limit value
is 0 and the Max Limit value is 350.

Take a note that the same values must be set in the corresponding VMID file.

                   Use the sliders of the Axis Control tab to check the translational movements of the defined geometry along
                    the axis.
Clicking the Spindle unit item highlights the spindle and displays the component properties.

During mouse rotation in machine simulation, a dot is displayed to show end-users the selected rotation center point.
                                                                                                                                                              261
Click the Y item in the CNC-Machine definition tree. In the element properties table, the orientation of the axis is defined by a
vector with three coordinates.

In this case, the defined translational axis is the Y-axis. Notice that the direction is defined according to the tool movements
along the axis. The tool movement in the positive Y-axis direction causes the saddle movement in the negative Y-direction.

     Saddle movement
     direction

                      Tool movement
                      direction

                                                                                                      ZY
                                                                                                          X

Therefore, the direction is defined with the following values: 0.00000 -1.00000 0.00000. This vector defines the direction of the
CNC component movement when the tool moves in the positive axis direction.

The saddle performs the Y-axis movements within the range of the minimal and maximal limit values. The Min Limit value is set
to -350 and the Max Limit value is set to 350.

Take a note that the same values must be set in the VMID file.

                                                                                                                                 20. Machine Definition
After the translational axis parameters, the geometry of the part performing the translational movement along the axis is
defined. In this case, this part is the saddle.

                   Use the sliders of the Axis Control tab to check the translational movements of the defined geometry along
                   the axis.

Defining the tilting table
At this stage, you can review the definition of the tilting table providing the B-axis rotation.
Click the A item in the CNC-machine definition tree.
In the element properties table, the Direction and the Center point parameters determine the
location and orientation of the rotational axis.
The tilting axis performs the rotation around the X-axis. Notice that the positive tool movement
around the rotational axis (positive direction of the B-axis) is performed by the negative rotation
of the tilting table.

                -

  +

                             ZY
                                 X

The positive direction of the rotational axis of the tilting table is defined by the right hand rule. Therefore, the axis Direction
parameters values must be the following: -1.00000 0.00000 0.00000.
The Center point parameters define the location of the axis relative to the CNC-machine origin. In this case, the tilting axis is
located 30 mm below the table. Therefore, the Center point values must be defined as follows: 0.00000 0.00000 -30.00000.
The minimum and maximum rotation angle limits are set in the Limits section: the Min Limit value is -100° and the Max Limit
value is 100°.

item has the same values, but they can change when Machine Simulation
of a certain part is loaded.

20.1.11 Defining the magazine
At this stage, you can review the Magazine part of the CNC-Machine definition tree containing features loaded together with a
CAM-Part, such as STL holders for Spindle, Fixtures, Target, and Stock.

        · CS1: The CS1 item defines the current coordinate system. If the coordinate
            system axes are added to the item in an STL file, the MAC is shown during the
            Machine Simulation.

        · TH_FXx: The CS1: The TH_FXx items defines the STL holders for Spindle turret.
        · Target_Stock1: The Target_Stock1 item contains the properties of the loaded

            CAM-Part stock and target. The number of Target_Stocks in the Magazine must
            be two times the number of tables (number of tables*2).
        · fxt1: The fxt1 item contains the fixtures defined in the Setup. The number of fxt
            defined in the Magazine must be two times the number of tables (number of
            tables*2).
20.1.12 Collision control
This functionality enables you to detect and avoid possible collisions between all components of the CNC-machine in the
process of machining.

The understanding of the CNC-Machine construction and kinematics is necessary for the collision definition. In case of Table-
Table CNC-machines definition, the machine construction precludes the collisions between the saddle part and the tilting and
rotary tables. The collisions between the sliding carriage and saddle are also precluded by the CNC-Machine construction and
kinematics. There is no necessity to check the CNC-Machine components during their movements for such collisions.

266
                                                                                                                                 20. Machine Definition
The collisions between spindle unit (with tool holder and tool) and saddle (with mounted tilting table and rotary table) are
possible. Such collisions have to be detected and avoided.
The cc1 item displayed in the machine definition tree and a table divided into two sections
in the bottom part of the Machine tab contain the collision control definition.
For cc element, the user can define some specific settings:

        · Blue Panel (Left Side): It represents the first group of elements which will be used for collision checking against the
            second group.

        · Red Panel (Right Side): It represents the second group of elements which will be used for collision checking against
            the first group.

        · "+": This option enables you to add machine elements to respective group. After selecting the elements from the
            drop down, click on Done.

        · "-": This option enables you to remove the machine elements from respective group.
The objects in the viewing area are color coded for each collision checking group and grey for non-selected objects. This feature
offers a better overview of the selected objects and the parts of the pairs defined in the collision checking group.

                                                                                                                                                              267
                   Use the sliders of the Axis Control tab to check the collision checking definition.When a collision is detected,
                   the contacting components are highlighted.

Every time the simulation started, a dynamic collision check (dynamic_cc) will be created automatically.

dynamic_cc automatically creates Collision Check which checks the collision between fixture_adaptor and workpiece_adaptor
on one side and stations with holders and tools are on another side.
20.1.13XML file structure
This topic describes the XML file structure for all machines which are made according to the old definition.
The lines of code are explained below:
<?xml version="1.0" encoding="UTF-8" ?>

              The first line of the xml should include the version and the encoding.
<machine_data name="Table_table" version="1.8" units="metric">

              Machine_definition: It delimited the whole machine structure, so it should be added as a second line in xml.
<view_transform initialvalue="1.00000000,
0.00000000,0.00000000,0.00000000,0.00000000,1.00000000,
0.00000000,0.00000000,0.00000000,0.00000000,1.00000000,
0.00000000,0.00000000,0.00000000,0.00000000,1.00000000"/>

              view_transform: This entity specifies a particular view adjustment transformation matrix. As it refers to the whole
              machine, it can only reside within the scope of the machine_data entity.
<axis id="X" type="translation" x="1.00000000" y="0.00000000" z="0.00000000"
minvalue="-380.000000" maxvalue="380.000000" valuetype="cont" initial_value="0.000000">
<axis id="A" type="rotation" x="-1.00000000" y="0.00000000" z="0.00000000"
minvalue="-110.000000" maxvalue="110.000000" valuetype="cont" initial_value="0.000000"
rzx="0.00000000" rzy="0.00000000" rzz="-30.00000000">

          · axis id:

        · axis: It is the start tag of the axis definition.
        · id: It is used to define the name of the axis.
        · Type: It can be rotation or translation.
        · x, y, z: It is used to define the direction of the translation axis.
268
                                                                                         20. Machine Definition

· rzx, rzy, rzz:

· For a rotation axis, these values specify the rotation center point of the axis.

· For a translation axis, the vector (rzx, rzy, rzz) specifies the start value of the axis (initial value at startup).

· minvalue: It represents the minimum value allowed for this axis. A value underflow results in an exception.

· maxvalue: It represents the maximum value allowed for this axis. A value overflow results in an exception.

· valuetype: The valuetype axis attribute can have the following values:
   cont, discrete_list, discrete_stepped.

· discrete_list: For discrete_list, the values are specified in the neighboring values attribute, separated by commas.

· discrete_stepped: For discrete_stepped, the step value is passed through the step attribute.

· initial_value: It represents the initial axis position when the machine is loaded. By default, the initial position is 0. The
   value can be between the minimum and maximum limits.

In the axis tree you can add geometries in *.stl format. The geometries can have the properties listed below. When you add
geometries, you must be sure that you are adding them in the correct tree structure.

<geometry name="tilting table" geo="table_table - b_axis-1.stl"                                 clrr="1.00000000"
                                                                                                reflectivity="0.0"
clrg="0.50196081"     clrb="0.00000000"                  alpha="0.0"

reflectivityBitmapFileName="" objtype="geometry"/>

· geometry_name:

· geometry: It is the first string after the start tag which defines the entity.

· name: It is the id of the geometry.

· geo: The file selected as a path can use any relative path (In this case the geometry will be checked relative to the .xml)
   or full path.

· clrr, clrg, clrb: It is used to define the RGB color.

· alpha: It represents the transparency between 0 (Opaque 0%) and 1 (transparent 100%).

· reflectivity: It represents the reflectivity value between 0 (0%) and 1 (100%).

· reflectivityBitmapFileName: It is used to define the path (relative or full) to the picture (.jpg, .png, .bmp) used for
   reflectivity. The relative path is relative to the .xml file.

· objtype: Based on requirements, the geometry can have a simple property geometry or a special type.

          · transform_id:

· transform: It is the start tag of the transform option.

· id: It is used to define the name of the transform. (turret_transform, station_transformXXXX, adaptor_
   transformXXXX, holder_transform, tool_adaptor1, fixture_transform1, workpiece_transform1)

                                                                                   271
        · turret_transformX: It represents the turret transformation.
        · station_transformXXXX: It represents the station transformation.
        · adaptor_transformXXXX: It represents the adaptor transformation.
        · fixture_transformX: It represents the fixture transformation.
        · workpiece_transformX: It represents the workpiece transformation.
        · fixture_adaptorX: It represents the fixture adaptor.
        · workpiece_adaptorX: It represents the workpiece adaptor.

        · initialvalue: It contains a 4x4 matrix which allows you to move the object in the station_transform tree, adaptor_
            transform tree and holder_transform tree.

          · geometry name="tool":

        · geometry: It is the first string after the start tag. It defines the entity used.
        · name: It is used to define the id of the tool.
        · geo: It is used to define the tool geometry (with tool holder).

          · geometry name="tool_tip_1":

        · geometry: It is the first string after the start tag. It defines the entity used.
        · name: It is used to define the id of the tool tip.
        · geo: It is used to define the tool tip geometry.
        · cuttr, cuttg, cuttb: It is used to define the RGB color of the tool flute.
        · noncuttr, noncuttg, noncuttb: It is used to define the RGB color of the tool shaft.
        · arborr, arborg, arborb: It is used to define the RGB color of the arbor.
        · holderr, holderg, holderb: It is used to define the RGB color of the holder.
        · alpha: It represents the transparency between 0 (opaque 0%) and 1 (transparent 100%).
272
                                                                                                                        20. Machine Definition
· reflectivity: It represents reflectivity value between 0 (opaque 0%) and 1 (transparent 100%).

· reflectivityBitmapFileName: It represents the name of file which contains a picture used to simulate the reflection of
   an environment. The file format must be *.bmp. The picture size must be a power of 2 512x512 (suggested).

<geometry name="mh_Housing" geo="table_table - base-1.stl" clrr="0.75294125" clrg="0.75294125"

objtype="geometry"/>

· geometry name:

· name: It is used to define the machine housing geometry and can be switched on/off using the show/hide machine
   housing button.

<collcheck id="cc1" name="cc1" group1="Spindle unit,tool" dynamic1="true" dynamic2="true"
active="true" group2="Rotary table,saddle,tilting table"/>

· collcheck: It is used define a collision check rule. A collision check is always performed for the objects from the first
   group against the objects from the second group.

· id: It is used to define the name of the collision check rule. This name must be unique within the machine definition.

· group1: It is used to define the name of the geometries to be used in the first group.

· group2: It is used to define the names for geometries to be used in the second group.

            Objects declared in the magazine should not be directly referenced in the machine's collision groups definitions.
            Only the mounting adapters (points) should appear there. For example, you have a mounting point head_
            adaptor checked against geo1, geo2 and workpiece. When a replaceable component is added under this adapter,
            all it's objects will be automatically checked against the three objects mentioned above.

By default, the basic geometries used in the machine are added to the tree and the properties are almost same as the geometry
entity. The only change is the object type.

     · magazine: It is used to define the machine's replaceable components, identified by their type and name. Currently, the
        only supported type is "head". Each head definition must be placed directly under the magazine entity and include
        any kinematic branches. The stocks are first taken from the machine, and then from the magazine. The items in the
        magazine are sorted alphabetically when the machine is loaded.

     · head name="TH_FX1": These tags define the STL holders for the Spindle Turret. The items are numbered according
        to the number of tools used for a certain workpiece machining.

     · head name="fx1": It is the start tag of the defined fixture in the group.

     · </head>: It is the end tag of fixture defined in the setup.

     · fixture:

     · objtype="fixture": A fixture object is important when chucks and jaws are used with cl commands. Some of them
        allow automatic clamping.

     · fixture:

     · objtype="fixture": A fixture object is important when chucks and jaws are used with cl commands. Some of them
        allow automatic clamping.

     · Workpiece:

     · objtype="workpiece": It is mandatory because some of the commands from cl need to sync the moves with it or for
        collision purpose.

     · Stock:

     · objtype="stock": Stock is a mandatory object. If the material mode option is selected, it will cut the stock material.

     · Toolpath:

     · objtype="toolpath": The toolpath is a mandatory object type and has two properties:

         · It is the object which draws the dynamic toolpath input after processing.

         · It can be used to insert wireframe entities from input .asc files called geo="toolpath.asc"

     · </magazine>: It is the end tag for the magazine definition.

</machine_definition>

     · </machine_definition>: It is the end tag for the CNC machine definition.

For the XML file structure for all machines which are made according to the new definition, refer to the InventorCAM Milling
Help.

3+2 Operations  21
 21.1 Start 3+2 Roughing Operation
 To add a 3+2 Roughing Operation to the CAM-Part, right-click the Operations header in InventorCAM Manager and choose
 Auto 3+2 Roughing command from the Add Milling Operation submenu.

 The 3+2 Contour roughing operation dialog box is displayed.

 Technology
 The two technology types available are 3+2 THSR Hatch and 3+2 THSR Contour.
 21.2 Geometry
 The Geometry page enables you to define the 3D model geometry for the 3+2 operation.

 21.2.1 Geometry definition
 The Target geometry section enables you to specify the appropriate Coordinate System for the operation and to define the
 machining geometry.

276
          21. 3+2 Operations

CoordSys

I nventorCAM enables you to define the Coordinate System for the operation by choosing it from combo-box or by selecting
it from the graphic screen by clicking the CoordSys button. The CoordSys Manager dialog box is displayed. Together with
this dialog box, InventorCAM displays the location and axis orientation of all Coordinate Systems defined in the CAM-Part.
To get more information about the Coordinate System, right-click the CoordSys entry in CoordSys Manager and choose the
Inquire option from the menu.

The CoordSys Data dialog box is displayed.
When the CoordSys is chosen for the operation, the model is rotated to the appropriate orientation.
The CoordSys selection operation must be the first step in the geometry definition process.
Machining surfaces
After the Coordinate System is chosen, define the 3D Model geometry for the 3+2 operation.
The Machining surfaces are the entire model or any surface(s) of the design model. If you have already
defined 3D Model geometries for this CAM-Part, you can select a geometry from the list.

The Show button displays the chosen 3D model geometry in the SOLIDWORKS
window.

     enables you to define a new 3D Model geometry for the operation with the 3D
Geometry dialog box.
The 3D geometry dialog box has the following components:

              Name: This option enables you to define the name of the geometry.InventorCAM offers you the Default
              Geometry name that can be edited.
              Configurations: This option enables you to switch between SOLIDWORKS configurations. Choose the relevant
              configuration for the geometry definition.
              CAD selection: This option enables you to select the 3D geometry with the SOLIDWORKS tools.

                   When an object is selected in the CAD selection mode, the CAD selection button changes to Resume.
               Facet Tolerance: This tolerance controls the maximum deviation of the mathematical representation from the

                                                                                                                                                            277
                original solids and surfaces of your model. The 3D model geometry will be triangulated and the resulting facets
                will be saved. The triangulation is performed on the 3D model geometry when you use it for the first time in a
                I nventorCAM 3D HSM operation. If you use the 3D geometry in another operation, InventorCAM will check
                the tolerance of the existing geometry. It will not perform another triangulation as long as the facets have been
                created with the same surface tolerance.
     enables you to edit an existing geometry.
      enables you to view the available geometries on the model and choose the relevant one from the list.

                     When you choose the geometry from the list, the related Coordinate System is chosen automatically.
                 Surfaces and Offsets
 This section enables you to set the offset as Global or Radial and Axial.
 The defined surface is displayed in the Surface column. You can apply offset on different surface groups. In the Offset type
 column, you can select the type of offset you want to set for this surface.
                Global offset: This is a 3-dimensional offset which expands the machining faces in all directions.
                Axial and Radial offset: The radial offset is a 2-dimensional offset which expands the machining faces in the radial
                direction. The axial offset is a 1-dimensional offset which expands the machining faces in the axial direction.

                     When you choose Global offset from the Offset type list, the fields of Axial and Radial offset are disabled
                     and when Axial and Radial offset is selected from the list, the field of Global offset is disabled.
 21.2.2 Stock
 The Stock tab enables you to define the shape, size and orientation of the raw material to be machined around the machining
 surfaces.

            · Stock definition style

                The Auto updated stock option from the list, uses an STL model to define the raw material.

278
                                                                                                                                     21. 3+2 Operations
              The Surfaces option, enables you to locally define stock by selecting the surfaces. This option must be used when
              you want to machine a pre-cast or pre-machined stock. In the Stock Surfaces section, selecting the New icon allows
              you to select new surfaces to define the stock. Using the Edit icon, you can edit the selected surfaces. The Show
              button displays the surfaces selected for defining the stock.
              Using the Bounding box option, defines a rectangular bounding box around the machining surfaces. This option is
              mainly used when the material to be machined is a rectangular block.
          · Tolerance
              This option allows you to set the tolerance value for the tool to deviate from the surface.
          · Shrink/Expand
              The options of Shrink and Expand allow you to expand or shrink the stock model by the given value.
          · Stock has undercuts
              This option enables the roughing strategy to identify any premachined areas or undercut areas in a stock. When
              this check box is not selected, the tool path is calculated for all of the stock resulting in a lot of air moves. When
              this check box is selected, stock slices take into consideration the undercut regions and the tool path is calculated
              accordingly resulting in time savings.

                   This option is not available when the option of Bounding box is selected from the Stock definition style list.

          · Detect rest material thicker than

              This field enables you to avoid machining the areas where amount of stock material is less than the specified
              thickness threshold value. You can define the thickness value in this field.
21.2.3 Fixtures
The fixtures can be defined only by faces in 3+2 operations..

Right click in the blank area. Clicking on Add gives you the option of adding the faces and the fixture that is defined in the Setup.
After the clamping fixture is selected, it appears in the Surface column, and cannot be edited from the available right click menu.

                                                                                                                                                              279
Clicking on Select gives you the options of selecting Surfaces, or Model. According to the selection, the respective dialog box
is displayed for selecting the surface, or model.

21.2.4 Pre-drilled Holes
This tab enables you to use the option of pre-drilled holes.

This option is used to specify the start points of the roughing cycle. The aim is to start the machining
within pre-drilled holes. InventorCAM accepts multiple drill hole positions and automatically selects
a suitable hole for proper sorting.
InventorCAM automatically creates ramping after the pre-drill hole depth. The height of pre drill
position should represent actual depth of the pre-drilled hole. If it is higher than the machining level's
range, pre-drill position is not considered during the tool path calculation.
Select the Use Pre-drilled holes check box to either define a new hole or select an existing one from
the list. Clicking the New icon displays the Drill Geometry Selection dialog box.
This dialog box enables you to define the coordinate points where InventorCAM executes the drilling
cycles.

280
                                                                                             21. 3+2 Operations

· Name

      This field enables you to define the name of the geometry. InventorCAM offers you the default geometry name
      that can be edited.

· Configurations

      This option enables you to switch between SOLIDWORKS configurations. Choose the necessary configuration
      for the geometry definition.

· Mode

      Use the Select/Unselect mode to add and remove drilling positions from the geometry. The Undo/Redo button
      cancels or restores the last selection. All the selected points are shown in the list at the bottom of the dialog box. To
      remove a point from the list, right-click its entry in the list and choose the Delete option from the menu or choose
      the Delete All option to remove all of the points.

· Select centers by

You can add drilling positions to the current geometry using the following options:

Pick position: You can define drilling positions one by one using the CAD point selection options. You can also
type the coordinates into the X,Y,Z dialog box and confirm each drilling point by clicking Enter.

3 Points on circumference: Usually, all curves and arcs of imported models are converted into splines by the
exporting CAD system. Due to the nature of spline curves or surface boundaries, you cannot pick a center position
like you could on a circle or arc. InventorCAM calculates the center position of an arc defined by three points
positioned on the spline edges. This facilitates the selection of drilling centers on spline surfaces.

Pick the first point ­ pick a point on the circumference of the circle or arc using the CAD

point selection options or type the coordinates into the X,Y,Z dialog box and confirm.

Pick the second point.

Pick the third point.

I nventorCAM calculates the center of the three points and displays the drilling position.

Multi-positions/Smart Face: With this option, you can select model faces. InventorCAM automatically determines
all arcs/circles located on the selected face and defines the center points as drilling positions. You can also use the
mouse to drag a box over the area of the model. InventorCAM automatically recognizes all arcs inside this area and
defines the center points as drilling positions. The options you have selected in Filter for circle/arc selection affect
affect the search when you use the Multi-positions option.

The option of Smart Face is unchecked by default. When any of the option of Filter for circle/arc selection is
used, Smart Face remains unchecked and is disabled. When Smart Face is checked, you can pick the face and
synchronization works on that entire face. However, if a filter is used selecting Smart Face, this option automatically
unchecks and disables.

All circle/arc centers: InventorCAM searches the solid model for arcs and circles and adds all center points as
drilling positions to the geometry. The options you have selected in Filter for circle/arc selection affect the search
result when using the All circle/arc centers option.

CAD selection: InventorCAM enables you select the drilling geometry with the SOLIDWORKS selection tools.
Check whether the filter options you have selected are correct. Click CAD selection. The CAD selection changes to
Resume. Select the geometry with SOLIDWORKS tools. Click Resume.

                                                                                             281
          · Filter for circle/arc selection

              You can use various filters to search circles/arcs on the model for drilling positions. InventorCAM selects only the
              drilling positions with the attributes set in the Filter options. You can select the following attributes:
              Include arcs: If this check box is selected, InventorCAM also includes arc centers in the search for drilling positions.
              This option can solve the occasional problem caused by imported 2D sketches that have circle entities cut to halves.
              It also enables you to place drills in filleted corners.
              Include points: If this check box is selected, InventorCAM also includes sketch points in the search for drilling
              positions.
              All circles: InventorCAM searches the entire model for circles. Arcs or broken circles are not included in the search
              result.
              By Radius: You can limit the search by defining a radius value. Only arcs and circles with the given radius are
              selected and their center position is added to the drilling geometry. You can define the radius value on the model.
              Click By Radius. In the Select radius by dialog box you can choose how you want to pick the radius from your
              model. Select the corresponding option and pick the radius from an existing circle/arc or use the 3 Points on
              circumference definition.
              On Z-Level: You can also limit the search to the Z-level. Only arcs and circles on the given XY-plane are selected
              and their center positions are added to the drilling geometry. You can select the Z-level on your model. Click On
              Z-level and select a point on your model using the point selection options. The Z-coordinate of the selected point
              is passed to the corresponding field.

          · Selected circles/arc

              When defining positions for the current drilling geometry, the following useful options are available:
              No longer can be selected: disables the selection of drilling positions already defined for the CAM-Part.
              Show the center points: the center positions selected in all drilling geometries of the CAM-Part.
21.3 Tool
The Tool page enables you to define the tool and the related parameters for the operation.

21.3.1 Tool
When the tool is defined on the Tool tab, it displays the following tool parameters:
Tool type
Number

282
                 21. 3+2 Operations

Tool Offset
Diameter
Modified Offset
Corner radius
Cutting
Outside holder
Turret

The following tool types are available for selection:

          · End Mill
          · Bull Nose Mill
          · Ball Nose Mill

Dynamic Holder Checking

Selecting the Check with machining surfaces check box enables you to consider the shape of the Shaft, Arbor and Holder
during tool path calculation in order to avoid collisions between entire tool and machining surfaces.

Selecting the Check with in-process stock check box enables you to consider the shape of the Shaft, Arbor and Holder during
the tool path calculation to avoid collisions between the entire tool and stock material.
Clearances

The Shaft, Arbor and Holder parameters define the offsets applied to the corresponding parts of the tool.
Force Change Tool

If two operations use the same tool, and the Force Change Tool checkbox is selected, the additional tool change commands
are displayed in the generated GCode. When this checkbox is selected, the tool returns to the safety position in place of retract
position once the machining is completed.
21.3.2 Data

The Data tab to display and define the Spin and Feed parameters.

Feed defnition

The Feed section enables you to define the tool feed for the Sim. 5-Axis operation. The feed value can be defined in two types
of units: F and FZ. F is the default that signifies Units per minute. FZ signifies Units per tooth and is calculated according to the
following formula:

FZ = F/(Number of Flutes * S)

                                                                                                                                                              283
The F/FZ buttons enable you to check the parameter values.
Spin definition
The Spin section enables you to define the spinning speed of the tool. The spin value can be defined in two types of units: S
and V. S is the default that signifies Revolutions per Minute. V signifies material cutting speed in Meters per Minute in the Metric
system or in Feet per Minute in the Inch system; it is calculated according to the following formula:

V = (S * PI * Tool Diameter) / 1000

Spin rate defines the normal spin rate used for rough milling. Spin finish defines the finish rate used for finish milling.
The Gear list enables you to select a Gear producing the spinning speed. The first parameter in the parentheses is a spin
range; the second parameters is the power. The gear is selected automatically according to the defined spin. Only gears having
the current spin value within their range are shown in the list. The Spin direction section enables you to choose between the
clockwise (CW) or counterclockwise (CCW) direction.
Cutting feed
This field defines the cutting feed rate of the tool.
Feed Z
This field defines the feed of the tool movements from the safety position to the cutting depth.

                   For Tap tools, InventorCAM automatically calculates the Feed Z (F and FZ) value according to the following
                   formulas:

                               F=Spin Rate * Pitch
                               FZ=Pitch

                   The calculated values are displayed in the Feed Z field. These values cannot be changed.
Retract feed
This field defines the feed of the tool movements from the material to the retract level. The default value of the Retract Feed
is calculated according to the following formula:

                  Retract Feed=Cutting Feed * 2

Offsets

Length offset number

This parameter defines the number of the Length Offset Register of the current tool in the Offset table of the CNC-machine.
Cutting conditions
This button enables you to update the cutting conditions defined for use of the current tool on the chosen CNC-machine
according to the parameters set in the Part Tool Table.
Click the Feed Control tab to display and define the feed control parameters.

284
                     21. 3+2 Operations

21.3.3 Feed control

The Feed Control tab of 3+2 operations is similar to the Feed Control tab of Sim 5-Axis operations, except for the following
additions that are available in 3+2 operations:
Rapid approach
Selecting this check box enables you to apply rapid speed for retract rate.
Rapid retract
Selecting this check box enables you to use Rapid feed rate for tool approaching in case when the tool path start point is located
in air outside the part and stock material.
Adaptive feed rate
Selecting this check box enables you to automatically adapt the feed rate of the tool depending on the engagement volume in
the material. Minimum feed rate % is applied in case of 100% engagement.
Feed rate for direct/spline links
This section enables you to define determined feed rates for direct links and spline links that correspond with the machining
feed rate.
When you select the Area links check box, you can define links between tool path at the same level. The links can be defined
within the group and between two different groups.
Selecting the Within group check box specifies the connection moves between the offset cuts in a single group.
Selecting the Between groups check box specifies connection moves between the different groups inside a single region on the
same cutting layer.

                   The options of Within group and Between groups are available only when the Area links check box is selected.

When you select the Links between slices check box, you can set the value of connection moves between the cutting layers on
multiple heights.
When you select the Links between regions check box, you can set the value of connection moves between the different
machining regions.

                                                                                                                                                              285
21.4 Levels
The options available on the Regular and Advanced tabs of the Levels page of 3+2 operations is similar to the Levels page of
the Sim 5-Axis operation. For more information, see chapter 5.

The levels page of 3+2 operations has fewer options than that are available for the Sim 5-Axis operations.
21.5 3+2
The 3+2 page of both the strategies enable to detect and process undercut areas in the specified range of processing angles.

Tilting Strategy
This section enables you to find the optimal direction based on the maximum stock/material that can be machined. Three
modes are available:

        · Automatic: Automatic machining searches the unmachined areas and processes them according to the maximum
            increment angle, maximum stock to leave, and maximum tilt range. Based on these parameters, machining is performed
            from all possible indexed directions.

        · Semi automatic: Semi-automatic mode enables you to start machining from a preferred direction. You can edit the
            values of maximum angle increment, maximum stock to leave and maximum tilt range parameters.

        · Manual: When this mode is selected, you can choose to machine only from the directions added by you or the tool
            plane. The part is machined in the chosen directions according to the maximum reach of the tool.

286
                                                                             21. 3+2 Operations

Directions

In this section, you can set the machining directions when the Tilting Strategy is not selected as Automatic.

Right click inside the direction table to display the available options.
Add

You can add machining directions using the following options:

        · Select: Selecting this option displays the Pick Edge or Face dialog box. Select the
            edge or face on the InventorCAM model. The selection is diaplayed in the Direction
            field. You can reverse the selected direction using

        · Along +X, -X, +Y, -Y, +Z, -Z: You can select the machining direction along the required
            axis.

        · 2 Points direction: Selecting this option displays the Select first point dialog box. After selecting the first point, click
                 . The Select second point dialog box is displayed. Select the second point and click .

        · Along user defined direction: Selecting this option displays the Direction dialog box. Define the direction vectors by
            its coordinates (dX, dY, and dZ parameters).

The table is populated with the selected directions. The below image is for
illustrative purpose only. The rows in the populated table can be moved up

and down using  .

Replace

This command is used to replace a machining direction with a different type
of machining direction. The selected machining direction type is disabled
in the right click menu.

Reverse

This command is used to reverse the direction of machining.
Edit

This command is used to edit the direction vectors.
Delete

This command is used to delete a selected machining direction.
Delete All

This command deletes all the machining directions in the table.
Max. Increment angle

For each unmachined zone, the tool is rotated in increments defined by Max. Increment angle value to find the best direction to
machine the entire unmachined stock without gouging the neighbouring walls.

                                                                                                               287
Max. Stock to leave
In this parameter, the defined value works according to the iterative algorithm. After each iteration it finds the next unmachined
area and finds an indexed direction to machine the area. If the area detected is smaller than the Max. stock detection value, the
tool path calculation is terminated.
Max. Tilt angle
The value specified in this field, is the angle from the main machining direction in which the tilt search is conducted. It depends
on the machine kinematics and the work-holding.
21.5.1 Axial shift
This tab of both 3+2 operations is similar to the Advanced tab of the Tool axis control page of Sim 5-Axis operations. For 3+2
operations, only the options shown in the below image are available. For more information see topic 7.2.1.

21.6 Passes

21.6.1 Passes
The Passes tab of both 3+2 THSR Hatch and 3+2 THSR Contour technologies allow you to define following parameters:
Step down
The option of Step down enables you to define the distance between two Z-Levels.
The option of Number of slices enables you define the number of slices between Z-top and Z-bottom.

288
                                                                                            21. 3+2 Operations

Step over

The option of Maximum step over defines the maximum distance between two consecutive cuts.

Using the option of In % of tool diameter, the step over value can be defined as percentage of tool diameter.

The option of Scallop enables you to define the cusp height of the machined surface. The Scallop parameter corresponds to
the Maximum step over parameter.

Limits

The limits are the highest and lowest Z-positions for the tool ­ the range in which it can move.Two options are available for the
limit height. Both the options take into consideration the given geometries that are present in the operation.

When the option of Automatic is selected, machining heights are detected automatically by InventorCAM. When the option of
Stock is selected, only stock height is detected. When the option of Target and Stock is selected, height of stock and machining
surface is detected.

The option of Automatic heights must be used for Automatic and Semi-automatic tilting and the User-defined heights must be
used only for Manual tool tilting mode. InventorCAM recommends use of Automatic option.

In the option of User-defined, you can define the limits according to the Outside holder length i.e. maximum height is limited
to the tool's outside holder length or for every direction, the maximum height is limited to the value defined in the Manual field.

Cut tolerance

The Cut tolerance parameter defines the tool path accuracy. This parameter defines the chordal deviation between the machining
surface and the tool path; the tool path can deviate from the surface in the range defined by the Cut tolerance. You can type the
value manually or adjust it using the slider. A smaller Cut tolerance value gives you more tool path points on the drive surface
resulting in more accurately generated tool path. The result is a better surface quality, but the calculation time is increased. A
greater Cut tolerance value generates less points on the tool path. After the machining, the surface finish quality is lower but the
calculation is much faster.

Draft/Machining angle

These parameters enable you to draft the cutting layers outwards from bottom up so that it results in a conical opening for the
roughed pocket. Machining angle is not available for contour technology.

Closed offset

When contour technology is selected, this check box enables you to use the closed offset pattern even on an open pocket part.
21.6.2 Sorting

The Sorting tab provides the following options:
Cutting method

This option enables you to define how the cuts are connected. You have three choices: Zigzag, One Way, and Spiral.

                                                                                                                    289
        · Zigzag: When Zigzag is selected from the list, the tool starts at one end of the surface, steps over at the end of the
            surface and continues machining in the opposite direction.

        · One Way: When One way is selected from the list, the start and end point of one cut are not the same. Once the end
            of the drive surface is reached, tool retracts and moves all the way back to begin at the initial side of the drive surface.

        · Spiral: With the Spiral option, InventorCAM transforms standard offset roughing tool path into a spiral cut. This
            option is available only with the contour technology.

Direction for one way machining
This option allows you to define the moving direction of the tool on the part depending on the rotation direction of the
machine spindle.
When Direction one is chosen, the tool movement and the spindle rotation have the same direction. When Direction two is
chosen, the tool movement is opposite to the spindle rotation.
Machine by
This option enables you to define the machining order for a 3+2 operation. The Machine by list enables you to choose the order
of machining of certain areas; it defines whether the surface will be machined by Lanes or by Regions.
The generated tool path usually has a topology of multiple contours (lanes) on the drive surfaces. When the tool path is
generated in many zones, it might be preferable to machine all the regions independently.
Intermediate slices
When the Intermediate slices check box is selected on the Adaptive stepdown tab, this option enables you to specify the order
of intermediate slices machining.
When After each depth step option is selected from the list, intermediate slices start to be processed after each regular slice.
When After last depth step option is selected from the list, the process of intermediate slices machining starts after the last
regular slice processing.
Reverse cutting order
When this check box is selected, it enables you to change the sequence of machining within one tool path group. This option is
available only with the contour technology.
Start point
This option allows you to define desired start position for the roughing tool path. The actual start position is located on the
closest cut to the user defined point. This option is available only with the contour technology.

290
                                                                          21. 3+2 Operations

Selecting this check box displays the Start Point parameters dialog box.

For closed contours, the Start point option enables you to define a new position of the start point of the first cut. The position
is defined along a cut. The start points of the next cuts are determined automatically, taking into account the start point location
and the cutting strategy. Select the Use Start point check box to enable the dialog box options. Vector coordinates can be set by
numeric values, or the direction can be picked on the model using .

The Start point option does not typically define the exact tool path start point.

21.6.3 Adaptive stepdown

The Adaptive stepdown tab allows you to set the following parameters:

First/final stepdown
An additional depth step value at the first and last step down position can be defined for the beginning and end of an operation.
User-defined cut levels
The cut levels can be edited manually and inserted in the User-define cut levels table. Type the
Z-levels of your choice into the Z column of the table. The values will be sorted in the decreasing
order. You can also pick up the Z level by right clicking in the table and selecting Pick Z option.
Select a coordinate dialog box is displayed.
Intermediate slices
Selecting this check box enables you to define Constant Stepdown or Number of slices parameters.
When you select the Constant Stepdown option, starting from the Upper level, InventorCAM uses the specified step down to
calculate each successive Z-level.

                                                                                                                                                              291
When the Number of slices option is selected it enables you to define the number of slices by which the step down is performed
between two roughing cuts. The distance of the slices is determined automatically.
Machine flatlands
Machine flatlands enables you to machine the flat areas of the machining surfaces. Flat areas like parting surfaces can be
machined using this option. During Roughing when a flat area cannot be machined because it is between 2 depth step levels,
enabling this option identifies these flat areas and applies additional cuts.
The Minimum and Maximum width of the flat area that needs to be detected can be defined in their respective fields.
21.6.4 Smoothing
The Smoothing tab of 3+2 THSR Hatch provides the following options:

Final contour pass
Selecting this check box, allows you to get additional cuts along the part boundaries. It helps to remove any uncut cusps that are
left after the rough cycle. You can select between the option of All slices or Last slice. When you select Last slice, the Spacing
field allows you to offset regular rough passes from final contour pass. The spacing value should be less than or equal to the
tool radius.
Smoothing
Selecting the Smooth final contour check box allows you to create fillets in the sharp corners of the outer contour. The radius
of the fillet is to be entered in the Smooth distance/stepover field. Selecting the Smooth connections check box allows you to
round the connection segments between regular roughing cuts. You can define the arc radius in the Radius field.
Filtering
This section allows you to remove tool path regions based on their size. If width of a tool path region is less than or equal to
aspecified treshold, then this region is filtered out.
When Inscribed circle is selected from the list, InventorCAM automatically creates an inscribed circle to prevent the tool from
entering extremely tight area of the geometry.
When Diagonal length is selected, InventorCAM creates a bounding box with a specified diagonal length around the geometry
to prevent the tool from entering extremely tight area of the geometry.
The option of Threshold value in % of the tool diameter allows you to define the diameter of the inscribed circle or the diagonal
length of the bounding box in terms of percentage of the tool diameter.

292
                                                                  21. 3+2 Operations

The Smoothing tab of 3+2 Contour provides the following options:

Smooth corners
Selecting this check box creates a fillet in the sharp corners of the tool path.
Smooth final contour
Selecting this check box creates a fillet in the sharp corners of outer contour.
Smooth distance/stepover %
In this field, radius of the fillet can be entered as percentage of the step over distance.
Smooth links
This option smooths the links within a group. The last segments of the previous contour and the first segments of the next
contour are trimmed.
Smooth link gap size/stepover
When Smooth links option is selected, this field allows you to control smoothness of the Blend spline link between neighbor
cuts within group.
Minimize links
When this check box is selected, the system tries to use part of the actual tool path contour for linking instead of applying
retraction moves.
Filtering
The Filter by list provides the option of filtering through Contours or Regions.
When Inscribed circle is selected from the list, InventorCAM automatically creates an inscribed circle to prevent the tool from
entering extremely tight area of the geometry. When Diagonal length is selected from the list, InventorCAM creates a bounding
box with a specified diagonal length around the geometry to prevent the tool from entering extremely tight area of the geometry.
Threshold value in % of the tool diameter parameter defines the diameter of the inscribed circle or the diagonal length of the
bounding box in terms of percentage of the tool diameter.

                                                                                                                                                              293
Remove corner pegs
Selecting this check box allows you to add an extra movement to the corners that removes the material left in the corners.
21.6.5 Point distribution

Select the Enable/Disable check box to activate the point distribution parameters.

When the Maximum distance check box is selected, you can define the maximum length of a tool path segment. Depending
on the cut tolerance, certain tool path positions can be closer than the set value. When the Maximum distance check box is not
selected, then the tool path position is influenced only by the cut tolerance and the maximum angle step.

When the Minimum distance check box is selected, you can define the minimum distance of a tool path segment.

The Deviation factor value defines the chordal deviation factor presented in cut tolerance to keep the distribution of output
points within range.

           Using the options of maximum and minimum distance may result in collisions. However, it does not impact
           length of the arc segment.

21.7 Link

The Link page enables you to define the way how the generated passes are linked together into a tool path of 3+2 operations.

21.7.1 Ramping
This tab provides options to define the lead-in motion for the rough tool path segments.

294
                                                                                        21. 3+2 Operations

Center cutting tool

When this check box is selected, it defines if the tool can plunge into the material through vertical approach or should start from
the side of the material through horizontal approach.

Ramp type

This list allows you to define the type of approach movement. The following options are available to define the ramp type:

        · Auto: This option is set as default. This indicates that the ramp type is selected automatically by fulfilling user-defined
            and collision-free conditions. The following sequence is used in the selection of a suitable ramp type: Line - Helical
            - ZigZag - Profile. For example- If the Line type causes collisions and cannot be applied, then the Helical type will
            be attempted.

           In case where all the ramp types are not suitable, ramps will not be created instead a vertical approach move
           will be applied. The ramp will rise above the current cutting layer on (Safety distance + Step down) distance.

· Line: In this option, the lead-in move is defined along an angular line.

· Helical: In this option, InventorCAM allows helical entry into the stock material, wherein the tool engages the stock
   with helical interpolation. If Helical ramp cannot be generated using the defined geometry I nventorCAM switches
   automatically to the Profile ramp. You can define either the Angle or Pitch on selecting the Helical option.

                   You can specify the range of the Helix diameters through the Min./Max. ramp diameter. In this case,
                   InventorCAM determines the most suitable Ramp diameter from the range.
        · Zigzag: When the length of the ramp is limited, this option allows you to get Zig and zag angular moves. A horizontal
            movement is used to approach the chain. The movement values are defined in the Ramp angle and Ramp length fields.

        · Profile: When this option is selected, the tool engages the stock following the tool path profile or contour of the
            part. The Ramp angle value must be specified to define the profile move and the angle at which it engages the stock.

Ramp angle

The Ramp angle defines the angle with which the tool enters the next slice or pass.

Ramp length (tool diameter %)

This option allows you to define the ramp length as percentage of the tool diameter.

This option is available only when Ramp style is selected as Auto, Helical, or Zigzag.

Min. ramp diameter (tool diameter %)
When this check box is selected, it limits the minimum diameter of the ramping movement to the specified value.

This option is available only when Ramp style is selected as Auto, Helical or Zigzag.

Stock clearance (Stepover %)
This parameter defines the start point of the ramp with the specified value as a minimum distance away from the stock.

                                                                                                                        295
Allow tool outside stock
When this check box is selected, it enables the machining around the part, and on the outer side of the stock in case the stock
has the same dimension as the part itself
Ramping priority sequence
This box allows you to set the sequence of ramping. Select the check box and click on a ramping type to enable the toggle
buttons. You can shuffle the order of ramping types as per your requirements.
21.7.2 Approach/Retract

This page enables you to define the tool approach and retract parameters.
First entry
This section enables you to define the first approach of the tool to the cutting area. InventorCAM enables you to specify the
level from which the approach movement is started.

        · From clearance area: The approach movement is performed from the specified Clearance area, through the Retract
            distance and Safety distance levels.

        · Use retract distance: The approach movement is performed from the Retract distance level.
        · Use safety distance: The approach movement is performed from the Safety distance level.
        · Start from home position: InventorCAM enables you to define the Home position for tool path linking that can be

            applied to the first entry links. Home position is a point from which the first rapid movement of the tool starts during
            the approach.
        · Use ramp/Don't use ramp: This list allows you to use ramp or not with the first entry movement.
Last exit
This section enables you to define the last retreat of the tool from the cutting area after the machining.
        · Back to clearance area: The retreat movement is performed back to the specified Clearance area, through the Retract
            distance and Exit safety distance levels.
        · Use retract distance: The retreat movement is performed to the Retract distance level.
        · Use safety distance: The retreat movement is performed to the Exit safety distance level.

296
                                                                                                                                     21. 3+2 Operations
        · Return to home position: InventorCAM enables you to define the Home position for tool path linking that can be

            applied to the last exit links. Home position is a point to which the tool eventually returns after the retreat. When the
            Return to home position check box is selected, the machining is performed as follows: After the last cutting pass, the
            tool returns to the Clearance area/Retract distance/Exit safety distance level (depending on the Last exit setting) or
            directly to the Home position (in case of the Direct option chosen for Last exit).

Home position

This section enables you to define the coordinates of the home position.

21.7.3 Links

This tab enables you to define the parameters of the tool path linking.

Area links

This section allows you to define links between tool path in the same level. The links can be defined within the group and
between two different groups.

Within group

This list specifies the connection moves between the offset cuts in a single group.

Between groups

This list specifies connection moves between the different groups inside a single region on the same cutting layer. The following
options are available in these two lists:

        · Direct: This option enables you to apply straight line connection between the passes on the shortest way without any
            retracting movements.

        · Blend spline: This option enables you to apply tangential arcs connection between the passes.

        · Retract to feed distance: This option enables you to apply straight line connection between the passes with retracting
            tool to the specified feed distance.

        · Retract to rapid distance: This option enables you to apply straight line connection between the passes with retracting
            tool to the specified rapid distance.

        · Retract to clearance area: This option enables the use of straight line connection between the passes with retracting
            tool to the specified clearance area.

Use ramp/Don't use ramp

This list allows you to use ramp or not with the first entry movement.

Links between slices

This section enables you to specify connection moves between the cutting layers on the multiple heights. The following options
are available in this list:

        · Direct: This option enables you to apply straight line connection between the slices on the shortest way without any
            retracting movements.

        · Blend spline: This option enables you to apply tangential arcs connection between the slices.

        · Step: This option enables you to apply link type that contains retraction, connection and vertical approach segments.
            The connection is located in the tool plane between the slices. Each segment has its own feed rate.

        · Retract to feed distance: This option enables you to apply straight line connection between the passes with retracting
            tool to the specified feed distance.

                                                                                                                                                              297
        · Retract to rapid distance: This option enables you to apply straight line connection between the slices with retracting
            tool to the specified rapid distance.

        · Retract to clearance area: This option enables the use of straight line connection between the slices with retracting
            tool to the specified clearance area.

Use ramp/Don't use ramp
This list allows you to use ramp or not with the first entry movement.
Links between regions
This section enables you to specify connection moves between different machining regions. The following options are available
in this list:

        · Direct: This option enables you to apply straight line connection between the machining regions on the shortest way
            without any retracting movements.

        · Blend spline: This option enables you to apply tangential arcs connection between the machining regions.
        · Retract to feed distance: This option enables you to apply straight line connection between the passes with retracting

            tool to the specified feed distance.
        · Retract to rapid distance: This option enables you to apply straight line connection between the machining regions

            with retracting tool to the specified rapid distance.
        · Retract to clearance area: This option enables the use of straight line connection between the machining regions with

            retracting tool to the specified clearance area.
Hatch passes link
When the Technology is set to Hatch, this section allows you to specify maximum allowed length of hard links between parallel
roughing cuts. If actual length of the link is bigger than threshold value, retract link type is applied.
Use Lead out Arcs
The Use Lead-out Arc option creates Lead-out arc for each step down to avoid any possible tool contact with machining surfaces
or in-process stock which can lead to shatter marks or even collisions. All retracts will follow Air move safety distance. Generally,
Air move safety distance should be smaller than Retract as it is used by the tool to retract more than the retract distance, in
case of an obstacle in the way. If a link cannot be applied, the following fallback mechanism is used. If a Direct or Blend
spline cannot be created, InventorCAM tries to create a Retract to feed distance link. If a Retract to feed distance link cannot
be created, InventorCAM tries to create a Retract to rapid distance link. If a Retract to rapid distance link cannot be created,
InventorCAM creates a Retract to clearance area link.A link cannot be created if the originally chosen link is in collision or, for
the retracts links, if the Air move safety distance is not followed.

                  21. 3+2 Operations
